### Page 3

3
Cathay’s View市場策略思維
市場焦點
1. 市場焦點：
• AI資料中心疑慮衝擊科技股: 民主黨在期中選舉獲勝, 多位民主黨州長重提AI建設禁令。市場擔心將影
響AI基建擴張, 參議員重提「超級AI禁令」更引發市場恐慌。
• AI熱潮面臨獲利考驗: AI算力需求仍強, 然市場進一步放大檢視「AI投資能創造多少收益」。AI基礎建
設受阻打擊AI供應鏈, 但資料中心回報率成長仍有利產業前景。
• 就業數據與美國經濟: 美國10月ISM製造業指數升至55.5創近4年新高, ADP新增就業8.6萬人, 優於市
場預期。經濟表現強勁, 通膨仍具黏性, Fed下一步仍傾向漸進升息。
2. 後續觀察重點：
• 10月CPI(11/10)、PPI(11/13)、輝達財報(11/18)。
策略思維
1. 股市-類股走勢分化: 選後政策預期變化, 加劇類股分化, 可提高非科技股比重, 透過持股多元分散, 降低投資
組合波動。科技股可逢低分批布局, 但宜控制持股比例。
2. 債市-Fed仍傾向升息, 殖利率高檔震盪: 經濟走強, 通膨具黏性, Fed仍有升息壓力, 資料中心建設受阻降低

資金及舉債需求, 10年債殖利率回落, 仍維持震盪格局。
3. 匯市-美元高檔震盪: 美元指數近期隨美債殖利率回落, 但受升息預期影響而維持高檔震盪格局。

Cathay’s View
民主黨在參眾兩院皆取得過半席次 美股年末表現
資料來源: 270towin.com, 截至2026/11/4。 註: 統計1986年以來資料。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/4。
美國｜期中選舉結果出爐, 民主黨在參眾兩院皆取得過半席次
• 美國府會衝突與「否決先生」: 川普失去國會支持, 剩餘任期出現跛腳危機, 目前白宮已研擬「無國會施政」劇本,
可能透過行政命令與關稅延續政策, 川普更宣稱將動用總統否決權來阻擋不認同的法案。
• 民主黨的制衡與調查: 民主黨宣稱將透過國會調查川普家族與政府機構, 更可能全面封殺川普的司法與高層人事任
命。金融市場並不擔心分裂國會帶來的政治僵局, 這將會削弱川普極端政策或激進財政擴張的可能性, 反而降低市
場不確定因素。金融市場更加關注的是, 選舉結果將主導科技、醫療、能源等核心產業政策的重分配。

### Page 6

6
Cathay’s View
美國AI資料中心禁令
資料來源: Bloomberg, 國泰世華銀行整理, 截至2026/11/6。
• 隨著美國期中選舉出爐, 民主黨多位勝選候選人已經在當選演說中, 再度將AI、資料中心及大型科技公司的監管問
題搬上檯面, 包括俄亥俄州、維吉尼亞州議員及伊利諾州州長, 他們強調從資料中心到AI安全, 都不能交由少數科
技巨頭決定。
• 整體而言, 川普政府加速發展AI的方向不變, 但地方政府對資料中心的土地、電力及環境審查可能更加嚴格, 根據
UBS先前預估, 2028年的資料中心擴建可能有30%受到衝擊, 高盛預估有45%的影響。
資料中心｜資料中心建置將受到一定的衝擊
州長／德州Gina
Hinojosa(政見)
1. 暫緩新建資料中心: 暫停新資料中心興建及核准, 直到州議會制定完善法規, 保障居民參與
地方開發決策的權利。
2. 取消企業補貼、落實成本自負：要求業者自行承擔電力、用水及電網建設成本, 取消大型
企業租稅優惠, 避免成本轉嫁給一般民眾。

州長／伊利諾州JB
Pritzker(感言) 強調伊利諾州已制定全美最嚴格的AI監管規範, 科技富豪不能凌駕法律。
州長／賓州Josh
Shapiro(感言) 強調不能讓大型科技公司制定影響未來的規則, 地方社區應有最終決定權。
參議員當選人／俄亥俄州
Sherrod Brown(感言) 「I do not work for data centers.」（我不是為資料中心業者工作的)。
參議員當選人／南卡羅來
納州Annie Andrews(感言)社區決策應由當地居民決定, 而非華府的億萬富豪或資料中心業者。
參議員／維吉尼亞州Mark
Warner(感言)
AI雖有創新與創造產業的機會, 但也有負面影響, 從資料中心到AI安全, 都不能交由少數科技
巨頭決定。

### Page 7

Cathay’s View
2028年的資本支出可能受波及
註: 5大CSP業者指谷歌、微軟、Meta、亞馬遜及甲骨文。
資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/11/3。
• 在AI需求持續強勁之際 , 大型雲端服務供應商擴大資本支出已成市場共識 , 市場預估2026 年整體資本支出將達
8,000億美元, 2027年更有望正式突破1兆美元。然而, 隨著AI監管趨嚴, 加上部分州政府開始考慮暫緩資料中心興
建, 未來AI基礎建設的擴張速度可能受到影響。
• 短期而言, 預期2027年CSP資本支出仍具一定延續性, 但若地方政府陸續提高建置門檻, 2028年投資計畫可能面臨
較明顯的延遲或調整。後續應持續關注大型CSP的資料中心布局, 是否轉往政策較友善的州別, 或進一步擴大海外
投資, 這將是影響未來AI資本支出規模與成長速度的重要變數。
資本支出｜2028年的資本支出面臨下修壓力

Cathay’s View
GPU出租價格仍持續上漲, 降低折舊風險 雲端營收持續高成長
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/4。 註: 谷歌、微軟、亞馬遜雲端營收合計。 資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/11/4。
• 隨資料中心積極擴張資本支出, 市場擔憂未來AI投資與折舊成本的顯著增加, 將高於未來營收增速, AI投資回報率
下降。惟算力供給吃緊, 新舊GPU租金都持續上漲, 2022年量產的H100晶片, 折舊成本已接近攤平, 未來的租金收
入反而都將快速提升資料中心的投資回報率。
• 若由3大CSP業者雲端業務營收來觀察, 2026Q3合計營收已突破1000億美元, 2028年彭博預估更高達8932億美元,
若由於AI資料中心基建受阻而影響算力供給, 資料中心的投資回報率提高, 大型CSP仍是最大受惠族群。
科技｜GPU租金持續上漲、舊晶片折舊風險降低, 將提高資料中心投資回報率

Cathay’s View
Anthropic Q3營收168億美元, 已成軟體巨擎 Meta主業廣告營收受惠AI成長加速
資料來源: Anthropic預估, 預估期間為2026Q3, 截至2026/10/26。 資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/11/4。
科技｜AI應用擴大, 軟體及終端收入明顯增加
• AI應用持續由基礎建設向軟體與終端服務擴散, 商業化速度明顯加快。Anthropic第三季營收達168億美元, 顯示生
成式AI需求已由模型訓練延伸至企業應用, 軟體業者營收規模快速放大, AI產業成長動能更趨全面。
• 終端應用亦開始展現AI帶來的實質效益, Meta核心廣告業務受惠AI強化推薦、廣告投放與轉換效率, 營收成長進一
步加速。隨AI投資逐步轉化為軟體訂閱、廣告及服務收入, 有助市場焦點由資本支出轉向變現能力。

### Page 10

10
Cathay’s View
儘管10月FOMC按兵不動, 官員態度仍偏鷹派 經濟數據支撐高利預期,  抵銷近期買盤效應
註: Logan為今年投票委員, Barkin為2027年投票委員。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/31。
註: 2-4月受就業與消費數據走弱影響、7-9月受就業及製造業數據降溫拖累, 經濟驚奇
指數回落; 惟油價與通膨預期影響, 美債殖利率維持高檔。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/2。
• Fed官員延續鷹派: 除10月支持升息的兩名官員以外, 近期多位官員亦重申持續升息方向, 成員涵蓋今年及明年具
投票權的委員。Fed態度暗示的漸進式升息立場大致與期貨市場預估一致, 料未來1年仍具2~3碼升息空間。
• 經濟數據強勁: 美國第三季GDP、10月ISM、9月JOLTS等數據均優於市場預期, 以及PCE增速持續走高, 經濟驚奇
指數的反彈, 恐緩和近期科技股賣壓 、高利吸引力推動的債市買盤效應 。雖今年經濟驚奇指數與 10年債利率走勢

時有背離, 但油價高檔、通膨預期擔憂仍使利率走勢不易呈現趨勢性下行。
• 展望後市, 1)期中選舉後的中東局勢仍有高度地緣不確定性, 2)季度再融資計畫反映公債供給壓力, 3)預估2027年
AI信用債淨發行規模 恐持續擴張。在通膨、債務供給與資金需求 三重因素支撐下, 高利率中樞環境將為市場常態。
債市｜聯準會官員延續鷹派、數據反映經濟韌性,  利率大幅下行空間受限
官員 談話
Lorie Logan
(達拉斯,  10/31)
需至少再升息兩次 , 才能有效壓低通膨 。逐步調整
政策有助家庭、企業與市場參與者適應更高利率。
Alberto Musalem
(聖路易斯, 10/31)
有必要進一步收緊貨幣政策 , 並且需要盡快 。更早
的漸進式調整優於未來幅度大且突然的行動。
Thomas Barkin

隨失業率維持低檔、經濟需求具韌性且高於通膨目
標, 支持漸進式升息路徑, 以觀望未來變化。 0

Cathay’s View
融資需求居高不下, 2027Q1升至8000億美元 新增發債需求更偏短天期
註: 採12個月滾動加總(12M rolling sum)淨發行, 近一年淨發行約2兆美元。
資料來源: Bloomberg, Sifma, 截至2026/9/30。
財政部｜美國2027年Q1借款需求攀升, 短債供給占比將逐步升高
註: 單位為億美元。
資料來源: 美國財政部預估。
• 最新季度借款預估顯示, Q3實際淨發債規模7420億美元, 大致符合8月預估值(7390億美元)。季末TGA餘額9840億
美元亦高於目標9500億美元, 導致Q4淨發債需求較前次預估下修100億美元; 惟考量期初現金餘額影響, 借款需求
實際較前次預估增加240億美元。2027年第一季借款規模預估8000億美元, 財政融資需求仍維持高檔。
• 財政部維持「Bills優先、Coupon穩定」的發債策略, 短期新增融資需求主要由國庫券吸收, 以避免中長天期公債
供給進一步增加。此舉有助控制發債成本, 但也意味市場對短端資金需求的依賴度持續提升; 目前Bills占未償還市

場性公債比重約22%(截至9/30), 高於TBAC(財政部借款諮詢委員會)建議的20%水準, 未來若借貸需求擴大, 市場
對流動性事件的尾端風險擔憂及再融資壓力的關注度將進一步提升。

### Page 13

Cathay’s View
第三季GDP年化季增3.4% 核心PCE年增率升高
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026Q3。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9。
• 美國第三季GDP年化季增3.4%, 增速創一年來新高, 並高於市場預期3.2%及Q2的2.2%。除了個人消費強勁, 民間
投資成長顯著: 在AI投資熱潮帶動下, 設備支出對GDP貢獻創2011年以來新高, 為支撐經濟成長強勁的兩大因素。
• 9月PCE與核心PCE分別年增3.6%及3.1%。以核心通膨觀察, 除了房租價格走高之外, 扣除住房的服務指標「超級
核心」亦同步反彈: 能源價格走升帶動運輸服務, 醫療保健、金融保險增速降幅有限, 核心通膨仍具黏性。
• 美國經濟活動持續擴張, 消費與企業投資表現同步穩健, 展現經濟韌性; 與此同時, 通膨增速居高不下, 均將支持聯
準會維持偏緊縮的貨幣政策立場, 隱含未來進一步升息空間。

### Page 14

Cathay’s View
10月ADP新增8.6萬與前月持平, 服務業仍為主要支撐 JOLTS反映就業信心改善
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9。
• 10月ADP新增就業8.6萬人, 優於市場預期(7.5萬人)。主要由服務業帶動 (貿易/運輸/公用事業+3.9萬、教育/醫療
+2.8萬、休閒餐旅+1.9萬), 但專業與商業服務(-2.7萬)及商品製造相關行業仍顯疲弱。
• 9月JOLTS職缺數+33.3萬至752.3萬, 職缺/失業比同步上升; 從新增比例觀察, 政府部門+10.4萬職缺(約1/3), 勞動
需求屬溫和回穩, 非私部門全面性爆發。細項方面, 自主離職數(轉職信心)反彈、裁員率減少, 均為正向就業訊號。
• 雖美國勞動市場自疫情後的高峰放緩, 但目前低裁員率 、穩定就業成長仍反映就業韌性。隨市場對勞動需求走弱
的擔憂淡化, Fed短期內亦無須過度關注就業下行風險, 政策焦點仍將著重在通膨進展。

英國物價與央行利率 MPC委員投票傾向
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 國泰世華銀行整理, 截至2026/11/5。
英國BoE｜英央政策偏鷹調整, 市場上修升息次數
• 英國9月CPI年增率由8月3.1%升至3.5%, 能源與運輸價格仍是主要推手, 服務業通膨升至3.7%, 顯示物價壓力已不
再侷限於能源項目, 而有逐步擴散至內需部門的跡象。雖然失業率維持4.7%, 且職缺數持續下降, 但私人部門薪資
增速回升至3.0%, 10月綜合PMI升至53.1, 代表現行利率水準對需求抑制效果可能不如預期。
• 因此, 11月MPC投票結果由先前 6:3支持按兵不動 , 轉變為全面升息 。同步上修 2026、27年經濟成長至 1.6%和
1.3%。大幅上調通膨預期 , 預估將在2027年年中攀升至5%左右(不利情境下甚至可能升至 6.2%)。導致今年年底

升息機率提高至 50%附近, 2027年2月升息一次的機率幾乎底定 , 政策基調已由 「觀察能源衝擊是否短暫 」轉向
「預防通膨預期失控」。

英國公共部門淨借款占GDP比重預測 長天期利率
資料來源: OBR預估, 截至2026/10。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/3。
英國預算｜財政赤字上修與公債壓力仍在
• 英國最新秋季預算案延續 「財政紀律」主軸, 聚焦於降低家庭生活成本與支持企業投資 。主要措施包括向約2000
萬戶家庭提供一次性 £100能源補貼、將家用電力增值稅降至 0%的措施延長至2027年7月, 以及延長每公升5便士
的燃油稅減免與燃油稅凍結政策 。同時擴大服務業減免範圍 、提高酒吧與音樂場館商業稅減免 , 並將個人營業收
入免稅額由1000提高至3000英鎊, 銀行附加稅自3%提高至6%, 並加強稅務稽核以增加收入來源。

• 儘管新增支出相對克制, 英國財政前景仍較3月預算案略為惡化。預測顯示未來數年財政整頓速度放慢 , 對市場而
言, 這代表未來英國公債(Gilts)供給將維持高檔, 長端殖利率易保持在較高水準。

RBA調低成長率, 調高失業率與通膨預期 市場預期澳洲升息循環將更快見頂
澳洲｜澳洲央行如預期升息一碼, 但升息循環告終可能性大幅提高
• 繼9月按兵不動後 , 澳洲央行11月利率決議如市場預期升息一碼至 4.85%, 但同時出現3票反對, 同時聲明上刪除
「必要時進一步升息」的字眼, 市場解讀為「鷹派升息、鴿派暗示」, 本次升息循環可能接近終點。
• 總裁Bullock表示, 他們對成長、房市、勞動市場的下行風險權重高於通膨上行風險, 並主張考量已累積的升息與政
策時滯, 應等待更多資訊 。顯示RBA在打擊通膨的同時, 仍不得不向疲軟的經濟妥協 。考量升息循環已接近終點 ,
對澳幣的看法轉趨中立, 但對於澳債仍正向看待。

• 10月外資賣超9943億元, 創下台股史上最高單月賣超紀錄, 11月賣超3307億元, 累計賣超逾1.3兆元, 為新台幣近期
急貶主因。

費半跌破7月低點, 健護指數衝高 美債殖利率回落, 地產指數反彈
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/4。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/4。
美股｜選後政策預期變化, 加劇類股走勢分化
• 民主黨拿下參、眾兩院過半席次, 並在地方選舉大勝, 政策預期變化加劇美股類股分化。資料中心禁建風潮恐拉長
申請審核流程, 遞延AI伺服器訂單, 半導體股持續修正, 費半指數跌破7月低點; 相較之下, 市場預期川普政府激進的
醫療削減政策將受到制衡, 使得健護產業成為選後主要的政策受惠者, 資金明顯轉進。
• 另一方面, 若資料中心建設放緩, 對資金及發債的需求降低, 美債殖利率應聲回落, 緩解利率敏感型產業的評價壓力,
地產指數明顯反彈。市場資金由高估值科技股轉向政策及利率受惠族群, 短期類股輪動加快, 美股多元配置重要性
進一步提升。

AI需求強勁, 帶動AMD資料中心翻倍成長 AMD毛利率續揚, 顯示需求依舊緊俏
資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/11/4。
• AMD第三季營收達133.8億美元, 年增45%, 高於市場預期, 非GAAP EPS達2.09美元, 年增74%, 同樣優於預期。
其中, 資料中心部門營收達85.5億美元, 年增97%, 創下歷史新高。此外, 毛利率達56%, 年增2個百分點, 營業利益
率則提升至29%, 反映AI業務規模擴大正帶動獲利能力改善。
• 展望方面, AMD對第四季及2027年營運前景展現高度信心。公司預估第四季營收可達170億美元, 年增約66%, 不
僅高於市場預期, 也意味營收成長動能將進一步加速。執行長蘇姿丰亦看好2027年營收成長可望超過80%, 主要來
自AI GPU出貨擴大, 以及伺服器CPU需求持續成長。不過, 遊戲業務營收年減40%, 顯示非AI市場仍存在壓力。

外資在韓股拋售力道仍大 韓股與韓元近期出現罕見的股匯反向
韓股｜近期外資拋售力道不減, 但韓股與韓元出現罕見股匯反向
• 外資對韓股的拋售未歇, 且此次企業回購結束, 本土法人與散戶買盤力道有限, 加上近期市場對於AI類股的疑慮加
深。基本面敘事改變與籌碼壓力下使韓股持續弱勢震盪。
• 另一方面, 韓股與韓元出現罕見的股匯反向, 在大漲/大跌的波動日尤其明顯, 與台股常見的外資賣、股市跌、台幣
貶的典型路徑相反。研判原因為(1)今年國際化改革後, 外資可直接於在岸市場交易韓元, 對本土匯市影響更直接。
(2)目前韓股外資的邊際流量以短線基金的快錢為主, 買/賣股同時搭配匯率避險/解除避險。整體而言, 韓股能否重
拾漲勢仍端看市場何時對AI產業的疑慮緩解。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/4。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/11/4。

Cathay’s View
五中全會要點 官方救市工具
中國｜著眼換屆, 五中全會先為未來一年定調; 人行等金融部門則聯手公布穩市政策
• 中共五中全會聚焦「持之以恒推進全面從嚴治黨」, 強調黨性修養、從嚴治吏、正風肅紀與反腐敗常態化, 並完成
重要人事調整與紀律確認。政治安全與權力集中置於優先位置, 這使得政策環境更偏向可控, 而非積極擴張。在考
核與問責壓力下, 官員避險心態可能延續, 進而影響經濟政策的執行力度與市場預期的修復速度。
• 與此同時, 人行會同國家金融監督管理總局、中國證監會三部門聯手開會, 重點有二: 1)將股票回購再貸款利率下
調一碼至1%, 以及2)第三批證券基金保險互換便利(Securities, Funds and Insurance companies Swap Facility,
SFISF)互換便利達1000億元申請(近前兩次合計), 以引導更多資金進入股市, 回應市場對「穩市」的期待, 有助提
振信心。
資料來源: Wind, 國泰世華銀行整理, 截至2026/11/3。 資料來源: Wind, 國泰世華銀行整理, 截至2026/11/3。

大盤企業獲利增長仍舊疲弱 A股指數與評價走勢
中國｜央媽祭出政策嘗試托底, 但大規模增量政策暫且無望, 加上國際氣氛影響, 指數需時間築底
• 在五中全會的政策指導下, 政治穩定先於經濟發展, 短期內難見大規模增量政策。考量現階段, 社會融資的年增速
仍偏緩慢, 顯示實體經濟融資需求與信用擴張動能尚未明顯回升。大盤企業整體獲利年增速依舊偏弱, 獲利修復基
礎不夠牢固。這進一步限制了市場上行動能, 加上近期國際股市氣氛不佳, 也使得估值修復難以一蹴而就。
• 考量數據與治理取向, 1)短線行情偏淡、指數需時間築底, 因為大規模刺激期待落空, 加上國際氣氛讓AI硬體股跟
跌, 長期均線呈現空頭排列下, 大盤轉為防禦與避險主導; 2)中長線在黨建先於發展的邏輯下, 資金還是只能流向國
企色彩高的類股, 加以人行將股票回購再貸款利率再度下調, 這應能為具有股息誘因的金融股形成支撐, 是大盤守

勢下, 非科技股的另一選項。
資料來源: Wind, 截至2026/11/3。 資料來源: Wind, 截至2026/11/3。

公司簡介 
台達電為全球最大交換式電源供應器廠商 ，
為因應全球產業競爭加劇 ， 已逐步從關鍵零
組件製造商邁入整體節能解決方案提供者 ，
3Q26 營收占比：電源及零組件 (Power 
Electronics) 54% 、 基 礎 設 施
(Infrastructure) 33% 、 自 動 化
(Automation) 8%、交通部門  (Mobility) 
5%。近年 PC 產業已漸趨飽和，台達電積
極切入資料中心系統解決方案、工廠自動
化 、 樓宇自動化以及電動車及儲能應用 ， 由
傳統電源製造轉型至節能相關領域 ， 並藉由
自動化及被動元件布局提升生產效率 ， 有效
帶動獲利攀升。

主要客戶： 
主要競爭對手 ：光寶科及國外電源供應器廠
商與電源相關零組件供應商

張智皓 chihhao.chang@sinopac.com 
本次報告：法說會

台達電 (2308 TT) 
成長趨勢未變

永豐觀點 
3Q26 受到資料中心相關產品物料狀況不順影響，獲利不及預期，然預估4Q26 出
貨將轉順，獲利將回歸成長。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持買進：雖近期受到料況影響獲利狀況，然台達電受惠 AI 資料中心建置需求未
變，電源及散熱零組件規格不斷提升，台達電獲利將受惠於規格持續提升，營運能
見度佳，訂單持續累積。另一方面，台達電在電力佈局持續，SOFC 解決方案將於
2027 年開始出貨。評價方面，台達電為電源及液冷產品龍頭廠商市占率高，受惠
程度明顯，營運將進入成長期，故以2027 年稅後 EPS 56.88 元計算，目前本益比
約 26 倍，處於歷史評價區間下緣，考量台達電逐漸從過往的零組件供應商轉型成
為系統整合商的角色，有利於在產品市占及獲利能力的提升，且獲利成長性顯著，
故維持買進建議，目標價 2130 元(30X 2027-2028 平均 EPS)。

ESG 評析 
台達電企業永續評鑑整體為 A 等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向
平均得分分別為 B+等與 A+等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

資料來源：SinoPac+ ESG 評等 (依循 2024 SinoPac+企業永續評鑑方法學)

專業意見，對於評分結果及評估報告內容之完整性及真實性，不負擔保責任，亦不對閱讀或使用
本評估報告之第三方負任何責任。

### Page 2

2

台達電為全球 Power 龍頭廠商：台達電為全球最大交換式電源供應器廠商，為因
應全球產業競爭加劇，已逐步從關鍵零組件製造商邁入整體節能解決方案提供者，
3Q26 營 收 占 比 ： 電 源 及 零 組 件(Power Electronics)54% 、 基 礎 設 施
(Infrastructure)33%、 自動化(Automation)8%、交通部門(Mobility)5%。 近年PC
產業已漸趨飽和，台達電積極切入資料中心系統解決方案、工廠自動化、樓宇自動
化以及電動車及儲能應用， 由傳統電源製造轉型至節能相關領域， 並藉由自動化及
被動元件布局提升生產效率，有效帶動獲利攀升。主要競爭對手為光寶科及國外電
源供應器廠商與電源相關零組件供應商。 
資料中心相關業務仍為成長主軸 ：電源及零組件部門(Power Electronics)部門營收
1059.3 億(+10% QoQ，+40% YoY)，成長主要來自資料中心電源及液冷出貨，營
業利益 199.2 億(-1% QoQ，+22% YoY)，營利率 18.8%；交通(Mobility) 部門營
收 90.7 億(+10% QoQ，+7% YoY)，受電動車需求持續疲弱，營業損失 1.37 億；
自動化(Automation)部門營收 159 億(+2% QoQ，+19% YoY)，因物料轉順，營
業利益 7.69 億元 ， 營利率4.8%； 基礎設施(Infrastructure)部門營收 658.8 億(+5% 
QoQ，+25% YoY)，成長來自資料中心液冷產品持續出貨，營業利益 120.68 億，
營利率 18.3%。整體來說，台達電主要動能來自於高階伺服器電源及散熱相關產
品，在自動化略有復甦，交通部門虧損收斂。 
供應鏈料況及資料中心建置遞延影響，3Q26 獲利不及預期：台達電 3Q26 營收
1970.59 億元(+7.5%QoQ，+31.1%YoY)，因長短料影響毛利率約 1.7%，毛利率
34.1%(-1.5ptsQoQ，-0.8ptsYoY)， 不及預期的36%， 營利率16.6%(-0.1ptsQoQ，
+0.1ptsYoY)，稅後淨利 234.95 億元(-6.5%QoQ，+26.3%YoY)，稅後 EPS 9.05
元，低於預期的 9.98 元。 
4Q26 電源及散熱產品出貨暢旺：受惠於 AI 伺服器需求持續持續增加，先前遞延
訂單將出貨，研究部上修預估 4Q26 營收 2162.3 億元(+9.7%QoQ，+33.8%YoY)，
在價格調漲及部分庫存迴轉，毛利率35.5%(+1.4pts QoQ，+0.9ptsYoY)，營利率
18.5%(+1.9pts QoQ ，+2.2ptsYoY)，稅後淨利 296.47 億元 (+26.2%QoQ，
+71.1%YoY)， 稅後EPS 為 11.41 元 。 預估2026 年營收 7588.99 億元(+36.2%YoY)，
毛利率 35.5%(+1.2ptsYoY)，營利率 17.4%(+2.3ptsYoY)，稅後淨利 988.33 億元
(+64.4%YoY)，稅後 EPS 為 38.05 元。 
AI 零組件產品內容價值提升及規模效應下，台達電成長快速：展望未來，電源產
品方面，隨著伺服器對電源需求增加，Vera Rubin 單顆 PSU 18.3kW 產品已經出
貨。此外，正負 400V DC 電源產品已小量出貨，預估將於 2H27 量產，成為未來

主要成長動能之一 。 電源ACDC 電源模組產品潛在市場持續擴大將繼續成長 ，DC-
DC 產品需求明顯回溫。散熱方面，液對氣維持高市佔率，且整體市場潛在產值仍
持續擴大，營利率亦高於公司平均，獲利貢獻顯著。整體而言，台達電在 AI 伺服
器規格提升下，成為主要受益的廠商之一，且因台達電具備關鍵零組件自製的優
勢 ， 能滿足客戶需求及控制成本 ， 長期競爭力佳 。研究部預估 2027 年營收 1.03 兆
元(+36.4% YoY)，毛利率 36.4%(+0.9pts YoY)，在規模經濟效應持續擴大下，營
利率 19.5%(+2.1pts YoY)，稅後淨利 1477.5 億元(+49.5% YoY)，稅後 EPS 為
56.88 元。預估2028 年在 800VDC 電源櫃產品及散熱產品持續放量，且燃料電池
及儲能系統將陸續貢獻營收，預估台達電2028 年營收 1.4 兆元(+36.7% YoY)，毛
利率 37.2%(+0.8pts YoY)，稅後 EPS 為 85.21 元。

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 10 月 29 日

1 群益投顧 
台達電 (2308 TT) 公布 3Q26 營收為 1,970.59 億元、 QoQ+7.5% ； 因 提 列 庫 存 跌 價 損 失
影 響，毛 利 率 降 至 34.1% ，營 業 利 益 率 為 16.6% ；稅 後 淨 利 為 234.95 億 元、QoQ -6.5% ，
稅後 EPS 為 9.04 元 。 隨 缺 料 情 況 改 善、 Vera Rubin 產品 量產出貨 ， 預期 4Q26 營運
將有顯著提升 。 受 惠 於 AI 、 Data Center 領 域 需 求 成 長 ， 營 收 與 獲 利 維 持 成 長 趨 勢 ，
投資評等為 Buy 。

研 究 員 ： 王 美 珍   
iris.wang@capital.com.tw

3Q26 稅後 EPS 為 9.0 4 元 ：  
台 達 電 公 布 3Q26 營 收 為 1, 970.59 億 元 、 QoQ+ 7.5%； 因 提 列 庫 存 跌 價 損 失
影響 ， 毛 利 率 降 至 34.1% ， 營 業 利 益 率 為 16.6 %； 稅 後 淨 利 為 234.9 5 億 元 、
QoQ -6.5 %， 稅 後 EPS 為 9. 04 元 ， 低 於 預 期 。  
產 品 規 格 提 升 ， 電源 類 產 品 展 望 佳：  
台達電 為 AI 伺 服 器 電 源 解 決 方 案 主 要 供 應 商， 受 惠 於 AI 需 求 提 升 ， 帶 動
AC/DC 電 源 大 幅 成 長 外 ，CSP 客 戶 在 新 一 代 ASIC 伺 服 器 平 台 採 用 DC/DC
電 源 模 組 ， 亦 成 為 另 一 成 長 動 能 。800 VDC 與 ± 400VDC 預 計 於 4Q26 出貨 ，
2026 年 營 收 比 重 約 1% ，預 期 2H27 營 收 貢 獻 較 顯 著。  
水 冷 散 熱 2026 年 營 收 維 持 成 長 趨 勢：  
水冷散熱產品有水冷板、 Manifold 、 CDU 、 Sidecar 。目前出貨以系統類的
Sidecar 為 主 ， 歸 類 於 基 礎 建 設； CDU 則 歸 類 於 電 源 類 與 零 組 件 。 水 冷 散 熱
2025 年 營 收 比 重 為 9%，台 達 電 Sidecar 產品 2025 年 市 佔 率 極 高，雖 有 競 爭
者 加 入 ， 但 整 體 市 場 需 求 持 續 增 長 ，2026 年 Sidecar 營 收 仍 將 維 持 成 長 趨
勢，預估 2026 年 水 冷 散 熱 營 收 比 重 為 12% 。  
固 態 變 壓 器 為 長 期 成 長 動 能：  
SST (固 態 變 壓器 )目 前 已 少 量 出 貨，客 戶 為 中 系 CSP 、800V 高 壓 直 流 電 動 車
充 電 站，亦 出 貨 給 美 系 客 戶 測 試。SST 導 入 需 從 資 料 中 心 設 計 初 期 即 納 入 規
劃，預期  2~3 年 後 會 產 生 顯 著 的 營 收 貢 獻 ， 仍 持 續 改 良 產 品 規 格 中 。 
投 資 建 議 ：  
隨 缺 料 情 況 改 善、Vera Rubin 產 品 量 產 出 貨，預 期 4Q26 營 運 將 有 顯 著 提 升。

2 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

3Q recap - strong op leverage offset GPM pressure — 3Q26 net profits (+125% y/y) 
topped our/BBG estimates 35%/28% respectively owing mostly to stronger sales 
and larger degree of op leverage. Sales reached a record NT$32.4bn (+24% q/q and 
+83% y/y), 23%/12% above Citi/BBG estimate, which can be attributable to strong 
demand in edge AI, semi equipment and smart healthcare applications. GPM fell to 
36.7% (-1ppt q/q and -2.2ppt y/y) below Citi/BBG estimates of 38.0%/37.6%, 
reflecting higher component prices. Nevertheless, OPM expanded to 20.9%, 
underpinned by strong op leverage on higher sales scale. OP profits +151% y/y beat 
Citi/BBG forecast 33%/21%, the key driver to the earnings beat.    
Implications — Strong order intake is encouraging with 3Q BB ratio reaching 1.6x 
and NA particularly robust at 2.1x. However, we believe the stock's rich valuation 
limits further re-rating potential, particularly as a higher comparison base could 
moderate growth in 2027E. Continued GPM compression also reinforces our 
concerns over material/component inflation and the timing of price increases to 
offset higher costs. Advantech will host 3Q26 results call on Nov 4th. We will revisit 
our numbers and share key takeaways following management's updates.

Advantech
Valuation
Our target price of NT$670 is based on 35x 2H26-1H27E EPS, at the high-cycle of the stock's valuation range between 20-35x
since 2020, reflecting solid BB ratio, improving margin profile and ROE.
Risks
Key upside risks that could cause Advantech’s shares to trade above our target price include: 1) better-than-expected
marcoeconomic outlook; and 2) better-than-expected demand and margin profile. Key downside risks would include: 1)

slower-than-expected demand recovery, especially the China market; and 2) further deterioration of demand in US and EU
markets.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

研 究  報 告                
十銓(4967.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
深化 B2B 布局開啟成⻑動能 
 投資評價與建議 
2026年營運受惠記憶體價格上漲及產品組合優化，2027年隨著新廠投產、企
業級客戶導入及邊緣AI需求；若公司能維持穩定貨源並有效控制庫存成本，獲
利有望維持高檔。 
 重點評論分析 
受惠記憶體價格漲勢持續 ， 推升營收獲利 ：3Q26營收季增33.6%至101.4億元 ，
年增143.5%，受惠記憶體報價上漲、產品組合優化帶動毛利率上升至49.3%，
營業利益率44.5%，本期淨利36.18億元，稅後EPS 37.19元，優於預期。 
庫存調配、產品售價同步拉升，有望支撐獲利：終端市況需求呈現分化，其中，  
AI資料中心跟企業伺服器、邊緣運算等較高階需求強，約佔整體DRAM產出
70% ；另消費端換機需求受到零組件成本上漲壓抑整體需求， 惟高階電競超
頻與AI創作者在快速運算跟大容量需求下仍強。在原廠優先生產資源向HBM、
高階DDR5傾斜下，除了減少DDR4亦排擠其他應用市場產能，將使工控、網
通、⾞用等⻑週期性客戶，⾯臨缺料與成本上升壓⼒。尤其部分工控及網通應
用仍依賴DDR4。截3Q26季底庫存達172億元，其中，DRAM約佔75%、Flash
約25%，公司現⾏庫存策略係以伺服器、工控⻑單及高階B2C為主。時序進入
4Q26，公司雖並未給予財測，但基於庫存調配及產品結構持續優化下，推估
4Q26營收107.９億元，毛利率52.3%，稅後EPS 48.6元。 
 投資風險 
記憶體價格反轉與庫存跌價風險、終端需求疲軟

### Page 2

2 
| 營運概況 
受惠記憶體價格漲勢持續，推升營收獲利：3Q26 營收季增 33.6%至 101.4 億元，年增 143.5%，受惠記憶體報價
上漲、產品組合優化帶動毛利率上升至 49.3%，營業利益率 44.5%，本期淨利 36.18 億元，稅後 EPS 37.19 元，
優於預期。市場別部份，至前三季 B2B(企業暨工控應用)營收從去年同期的 24.6 上升至 64.6%，B2C(品牌暨通路)
則降至 35.40%。 
庫存調配、產品售價同步拉升，有望支撐獲利：終端市況需求呈現分化，其中，AI 資料中心跟企業伺服器、邊緣
運算等較高階需求強，約佔整體 DRAM 產出 70%；另消費端換機需求受到零組件成本上漲壓抑整體需求，惟高
階電競超頻與 AI 創作者在快速運算跟大容量需求下仍強。在原廠優先生產資源向 HBM、高階 DDR5 傾斜下，除
了減少 DDR4 亦排擠其他應用市場產能，將使工控、網通、⾞用等⻑週期性客戶，⾯臨缺料與成本上升壓⼒。尤
其部分工控及網通應用仍依賴 DDR4。截 3Q26 季底庫存達 172 億元，其中，DRAM 約佔 75%、Flash 約 25%，
在庫存量部份，DDR4 占比約 60.09%，DDR5 約 38.51%，主要較早布局 DDR4 庫存；公司現⾏庫存策略係以伺
服器 、 工控⻑單及高階 B2C 為主 。 進入 4Q26 ， 公司並未給予財測 ， 但基於庫存調配及產品結構優化下 ， 推估 4Q26
營收 107.9 億元，毛利率 52.3%，稅後 EPS 48.6 元。 
2027 年前無價格下行趨勢：展望 2027 年，管理階層表示除了三大原廠之外，包含中國系廠記憶體廠，目前產量
以及未來 1~3 年陸續新增的產量，預期整體供需平衡至少要至 2028 年下半年或以後，且 4Q26 至 2Q27 係為缺
貨最嚴峻時期，2027 年價格難以見到反轉。產能規劃方⾯，新北約千坪自動化新廠預計 1Q27 進⾏裝修及設備進
駐 ， 2Q27 完工投產 ， 整體產能預估增加約 50% 。 十銓將事業群分為企業暨工控應用(B2B) ， 以及品牌暨通路(B2C) ， 
B2B 以伺服器 、 IPC 工控及邊緣 AI 為主要拓展 ， 提供記憶體與儲存方案整合 、 客製韌體 、 技術支援及可靠度驗證 。
隨著 AI 推論由大型資料中心向邊緣端擴散，不同終端設備對溫度、功耗、可靠度需求多元，具備技術支援及客製
化能⼒的模組廠，有助提高客戶黏著度與產品附加價值。另在 B2C 業務方⾯，公司持續深耕 T-FORCE 電競及 T-
CREATE 創作者市場，同時，過去十年在消費性產品中，高階電競與創作者模組占比已超過七成；在消費者對高效
能、大容量及超頻產品仍有需求的情況下，有助降低低階 PC 需求疲弱衝擊。整體而言，2026 年營運受惠記憶體
價格上漲及 B2B 比重提高，2027 年隨著新廠投產、企業級客戶導入及邊緣 AI 需求；若能維持穩定貨源並有效控
制庫存成本，獲利有望維持高檔。預估 2027 年營收 436.8 億元，毛利率 46.1%，稅後 EPS 169.64 元，目標價
340 元(2 倍 PER*2027EPS(F))。

Document 194: 5.7万亿美元“灰飞烟灭”！一场“应已计价”的中选却引爆了“科技股灾”

Source PDF: all report pdf/5.7万亿美元“灰飞烟灭”！一场“应已计价”的中选却引爆了“科技股灾”.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-07
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

⺠主党⼤胜国会后不⾜ 24 ⼩时，华尔街上演惊天逆转：纳斯达克 100 创 2020 年 3 ⽉以来最⼤
单⽇跌幅， VIX 恐慌指数从历史平静基点暴飙近 70% ，美股整体市值⾃ 10 ⽉ 6 ⽇⾼点算累计
损失已达 5.7 万亿美元。散⼾夺路⽽逃、对冲基⾦跟⻛踩踏、量化策略机械平仓 —— 三路同
时砸盘， AI 板块遭遇清算式重定价，积累已久的泡沫忧虑，在⼀场选举结果⾯前骤然引
爆。
摘要：
积累已久的 AI 泡沫忧虑，在⼀场选举结果⾯前骤然引爆。
美国中期选举⺠主党⼤胜国会，本应是⼀个 " ⻛险出清 " 的信号 —— 股市最初也如此反应。但不到
24 ⼩时，华尔街完成了从庆祝到恐慌的戏剧性翻转。周四，纳斯达克 100 指数录得 2020 年 3 ⽉以
来最⼤单⽇跌幅，单⽇蒸发市值 2.3 万亿美元；⾃ 10 ⽉ 6 ⽇⾼点计算，美国股市累计市值损失已
达 5.7 万亿美元。 VIX 恐慌指数从 16.6 点飙升⾄近 28 点，创下史上从最平静起点出发、升幅最为
剧烈的波动事件之⼀。

导⽕索是市场对⺠主党政策⽴场的重新定价 —— 尤其是后者对 AI 数据中⼼⼤规模扩张的深度质
疑。这⼀政治变量叠加早已⾼度拥挤的 AI 交易仓位，形成了散⼾、对冲基⾦、量化策略三路同时
踩踏的罕⻅景象。这场抛售的破坏⼒之所以如此惊⼈，在于它⼏乎没有给任何⼈留下反应时
间，从散⼾率先出逃，到对冲基⾦跟进，再到量化策略机械性平仓 —— 在短短数⼩时内完成。
选举夜：从庆祝到困惑
周⼆夜间，⺠主党的压倒性胜势超出预期。据华尔街⽇报报道，投资研究机构负责⼈ Ben
Emons 坐在曼哈顿办公室，从晚上 9 点到午夜，电话铃声⼏乎没有停过。他⼀边接听客⼾来电，
⼀边在桌边做俯卧撑提神。 " ⼈们被打了个措⼿不及， " 他说， " 这是⼀个真正的冲击事件。 "
彼时，股指期货在初步计票时⼀度下滑，但随着⺠主党胜势清晰、特朗普公开表⽰接受选举结
果，期货迅速翻红。 Key Private Bank ⾸席投资官 George Mateyo 在家中边看直播边与同事发短
信，他的判断是：选举后出现激烈争议的⻛险已经消散，市场理应对此表⽰欢迎。

这⼀判断在周三上午得到了印证。建筑、零售等 " 实体经济 " 板块⼤涨，道指录得 4 ⽉以来最佳单
⽇表现。华尔街短暂地松了⼀⼝⽓ —— 以为选举的不确定性已经⼀笔勾销。
然⽽，这场反弹很快被证明是⼀次假突破。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

散⼾率先出逃，恐慌情绪蔓延社交媒体
周四的抛售，最先由散⼾点燃。
开盘后的头半⼩时，纳斯达克指数急速下挫，个⼈投资者的净卖出规模创下历史性纪录。据分
析机构 Vanda Research 数据，周四单⽇散⼾净卖出规模仅次于 2020 年 3 ⽉新冠疫情冲击期间的
两个交易⽇。摩根⼤通市场情报部⻔则指出，散⼾卖出量偏离正常⽔平逾三个标准差。
" 我早上 7 点就开始⼯作了， " 哥伦⽐亚⼤学⼯程系研究⽣、哥伦⽐亚期权与期货交易俱乐部成员
Raphael Monges 说， " 我看到市场开盘，纳斯达克跌得⻜快，然后是标普。纳斯达克太疯狂
了，所有 AI 股都在砸盘。我的⼿机开始不停震动，所有⼈都在盯着股票，全是红⾊。 "
旧⾦⼭投资者兼内容创作者 Kevin Xu 在周四上午 10 点半瞥了⼀眼⾏情，随即愣住了。纳斯达克
已经跌了 4% ，他的退休账⼾⼀夜之间蒸发了六位数。 " 什么都做不了， " 他说， " 只能看着。 " 在他
的社交媒体信息流⾥，其他散⼾投资者有的发表情包，有的⽤全⼤写字⺟发泄情绪，还有⼈贴

出了⼼理健康热线的电话号码。
加州 50 岁的散⼾交易者 Joel Rieger 没有在这波动荡中调整仓位。 " 昨天的投资组合遭受了沉重打
击，到处都是红⾊， " 他说， " 短期内会很痛。 "
对冲基⾦跟进，机构抛售⽆⼈接盘
散⼾的出逃只是序幕。随着市场⽅向确⽴，对冲基⾦和⻓线资产管理⼈相继加⼊抛售⾏列，⽽
另⼀侧⼏乎没有买家出现。
据彭博社报道， " 机构投资者已经退场，⽽散⼾和系统性策略正在激进抛售， "Vanda 全球宏观策
略师 Viraj Patel 在报告中写道， " 这种资⾦流动态势，在真正的市场压⼒时期之外极为罕⻅。 "
摩根⼤通全球股票⾼接触交易联席主管 Ron Adler 在现⾦交易台⽬睹了全程 ——当⽇交易量较平
时激增 50% 。 " 我们看到的部分是投资组合的痛苦和⽆差别抛售，" 他在给客⼾的报告中写
道， " 今天资⾦流向的坦率程度在⽅向上是合理的，但规模之⼤令⼈咋⾆。 "
据⾼盛 Prime 经纪台估算，基本⾯多空对冲基⾦周四单⽇亏损 1.3% ，属于两个标准差事件；科技

主题基⾦跌幅超过 4% ，年初⾄今收益⼏乎归零。动量策略遭受了 3.7 个标准差的损失。摩根⼠丹
利数据则显⽰，对冲基⾦总杠杆率⾃ 6 ⽉峰值以来的降幅，已是⼗余年来最⼤。
量化平仓接⼒，系统性卖压⾃我强化
当市场跌势确⽴，⼀系列量化交易机制被触发，将抛售压⼒推向新的量级。
野村证券跨资产策略董事总经理 Charlie McElligott 估算，周四盘中期权交易商的负 Gamma 敞⼝
约为每 1% 市场波动对应 70 亿美元 —— 即股市每下跌 1% ，交易商就需额外卖出 70 亿美元股票以

### Page 3

对冲⻛险。与此同时，波动率控制基⾦需要抛售约 610 亿美元股票，其中 250 亿美元来⾃周三尚
未完成的平仓；杠杆 ETF 的收盘再平衡则将再贡献约 470 亿美元的卖盘。
趋势跟踪量化基⾦（ CTA ）也在同步撤退。据 Vanda 估算， CTA 在标普 500 和纳斯达克期货上合
计卖出约 130 亿美元，持仓降⾄历史低位附近。
巴克莱估计，波动率控制基⾦单⽇削减股票敞⼝达 30 个百分点，为 2020 年 2 ⽉新冠疫情冲击以来
最 ⼤ 单 ⽇ 降 幅 。 " 与 客 ⼾ 的 对 话 告 诉 我 ， 他 们 在 这 个 时 点 进 ⼀ 步 降 低 ⻛ 险 的 动 ⼒ 更 强
了， "McElligott 写道， " 反馈的主题是：保住年初⾄今剩余的⼀点点收益，活着进⼊明年的新资
本和新⻛险周期。 "
下午 3 点，纳斯达克跌幅已达 4.5% ，部分交易员还在期待最后⼀⼩时的反弹。结果抛售进⼀步加
剧，标普 500 指数收于当⽇低点附近，录得去年 4 ⽉以来最⼤单⽇跌幅。
AI 板块遭到清算，损伤集中⽽深重

这场抛售的破坏⼒⾼度集中于 AI 相关板块。
存储芯⽚、光⽹络、数据中⼼及 AI 半导体等细分板块单⽇跌幅达 12% ⾄ 15% ，属于五⾄六个标
准差的极端波动。约⼗余个 AI 股票篮⼦均创下⾃ 2022 年 11 ⽉ ChatGPT 发布以来的最差单⽇表
现。其中跌幅最重的存储和光通信篮⼦，已较 6 ⽉⾼点腰斩，跌幅超过过去四年任何⼀次回调。
截⾄周四收盘，纳斯达克 100 指数成分股中有 57% 较⾼点跌逾 20% ，这⼀⽐例甚⾄⾼于 2022 年 3
⽉该指数本⾝进⼊熊市时的⽔平。
这场崩盘之所以显得格外戏剧性，部分原因在于它⼏乎从平静中骤然爆发。 VIX 在周四开盘时仅
为 16.6 点，低于⼀年均值；收盘时已接近 28 点。⾃ 1980 年以来，标普 500 在前⼀⽇上涨 1.5% 后
次⽇下跌 2% 以上的情形仅出现过 48 次，⼏乎全部发⽣在熊市或危机期间。
政治冲击叠加旧忧， AI 叙事⾯临重新定价
市场的剧烈反应，折射出投资者对 AI 投资逻辑的深层焦虑。
在选举之前，市场已对 AI 交易的可持续性存有疑虑：科技巨头资本开⽀持续膨胀、现⾦储备收

缩、数据中⼼运营商资产负债表上债务堆积。⺠主党的胜选则叠加了⼀层新的政治⻛险 —— ⺠
主党对数据中⼼扩张的强烈抵制，尤其是在德克萨斯、俄亥俄、佐治亚等已成为数据中⼼建设
关键战场的州，令 AI 基础设施建设的前景蒙上阴影。
美银分析师 Michael Hartnett 指出，在受访选⺠中，⾼达 76% 的⼈反对在⾃⼰社区附近新建数据
中⼼，这正成为 AI 基础设施扩张的严峻障碍。
Man Group ⾼级投资组合经理 Matt Rowe 表⽰，选举结果提供了 " 可量化的证据，证明公众对数
据中⼼建设存在真实的反感 " ，这 " 开始改变我对这些项⽬落地概率的判断 " 。 " 所有条件不变的情

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

Causeway Capital Management 投资组合经理 Joe Gubler 指出， " 总会有输家，并⾮每个押注在
资本⽀出上的⼈都能成为赢家。 "
另⼀边，提前离场的投资者则避开了这场⻛暴。 Jeff Muhlenkamp 在暴跌前⼏乎清空了基⾦内全
部 AI 持仓，其规模 2.75 亿美元的投资组合 11 ⽉以来基本持平，全年跑赢标普 500 。 " 我们卖掉了
昂贵⽽流⾏的，买⼊了便宜⽽被遗忘的， " 他说， " 当动量转向，我们受益。 "
市场寻找出路
在 AI 交易遭到清算的同时，投资者正在寻找新的避⻛港 —— 但这条路并不平坦。
早在选举之前，资⾦已开始向公⽤事业、必需消费品等估值更低的防御性板块轮动。 10 ⽉份，

成⻓股跑输价值股达 10 个百分点，为 2001 年以来最⼤单⽉差距。然⽽周四的暴跌表明，这些新
宠板块同样未能抵御科技股崩盘的冲击波。
部分投资者则提前布局，得以全⾝⽽退。基⾦经理 Jeff Muhlenkamp 在暴跌前已清仓⼏乎所有 AI
持仓，其 2.75 亿美元的投资组合 11 ⽉基本持平，年初⾄今跑赢标普 500 。 " 我们卖掉了贵的、热
⻔的，买⼊了便宜的、被遗忘的， " 他说， " 当动量转向，我们受益。 "
周五，芯⽚股率先反弹，纳斯达克 100 指数⼀度回升逾 2.6% ，但各主要股指全周仍以下跌收
场，标普 500 周跌幅为 2.68% 。 Bright Trading 的资深交易员 Sinisa Sorgic ，当天做空台积电美
股，却因后者盘中反弹逾 7.8% ⽽ " 被烧伤 " 。他总结这⼀周的感受：" 昨天是⼀场⾎战。现在市场
太疯狂了，根本不敢全⼒冲进去抄底。 "
市场分歧已然浮现。 Key Private Bank 的 Mateyo 认为，州级法规会延缓、但不会阻⽌数据中⼼

的建设，公众对 AI 的疑虑或将随着实际使⽤的普及⽽逐步消退。 Wellington Management 的
Casaletto 则态度审慎，认为 AI 扩张速度放缓的趋势已经确⽴。
这场⻛暴究竟是 AI ⽜市中期的⼀次剧烈震荡，还是更深层周期转换的开端，华尔街尚⽆定论。但
⼀个现实已经清晰：当政治⻛向与拥挤仓位同时转向，市场所能提供的缓冲，远⽐想象中要薄
得多。

주가 추이

견조한 판매 확대로 매출 견조, 이익은 시장 기대치 하회 예상 
3Q26 동사 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 33.6조원(+17.3% 이하 Y oY)과 2.39조
원(+63.6%) 기록 전망. 3Q 매출액은 친환경차 중심 신차 확대 통해 두 자릿수 
성장세를 기록. 3Q 판매량은 도매 기준 84.3만대(+7.6% 이하 Y oY)로 전년 동
기 대비 성장 폭을 확대했고, 특히 친환경차 판매량 29.8만대(+45.9%)를 기록
하며 이번 분기 매출 성장에 절대적으로 기여.  
다만, 3Q 수익성은 4~5월 상승했던 원자재 투입 영향과 ‘경제형EV 믹스’가 더
욱 확대(13.2% → 14.5%)되면서 단기 수익성에 부정적 작용하며 시장 기대치 
2.5조원을 다소 하회할 전망. 그러나, 기말 환율이 1,356원/$ 수준으로 낮 아 지
면서 전년 동기 수 익 성 악화에 크게 영 향 을 미쳤던 판매보증비 외화환산손익 
영향이 개선(1.6%p 수준) 기대되며, 관세 부담 또한 북미 HEV 확대로 전년 동
기 대비 약 4,500억원(2.0%p) 감소하면서 부정적 수익성 영향 만회 전망.  
유럽은 점유율 확대, 북미는 수익성 개선으로 
올해 북미 HEV와 유럽 BEV 중심 신차 출시 및 물량 확대가 본격화되면
서, ’27년에는 이익 성장 기여도가 확대될 것으로 전망. 미국 조 지 아 텔 루 라 이
드 HEV 신규 생산(2.8만대) 및 HMGMA 스 포 티 지HEV신규 생산(1.0만
대) 등 수 요 가 높은 북미 현 지 에 서 HEV 물량 확대 중으로 4Q에도 텔루라이
드 HEV와 스포티지 HEV의 현지 생산/판매 확대를 비롯하여 셀토스 HEV 
출시로 ’27년 북미 중심 HEV 신차 물량 증가 및 믹스 개선 기대. 유럽은 강한 
BEV 수요가 지속되면서 중국 업체들의 공세 강화 중이나 동사의 물량 확대 또
한 견조. 3Q EV3 2.9만대, EV5 2.2만대 등 유럽 BEV 수요 대응 전략 주효. ’27
년에는 K4/셀토스 및 쏘렌토 등 HEV 신차 중 심 의 친환경차 PT 믹스 다양화
로 유럽 내 물량 확대 흐름 지속 전망 
물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결, 기말 배당 매력도 부각 
’27년 동사 영업이익 추 정 치 는 11.1조원(+13.5%)으로 증익 지속 전망. 최근 
환율 추세 고려 시 내년 수익성에 부 정 적 영향 확대 가능성 존재하나, 올해 확
인된 신차 중심 물량 확대를 통해 부정적 환율 영향을 상쇄할 것 으 로 기대. 
12M EPS 추정치 하향(-2.9%) 및 상 반 기 적 용 한 코스피 프리미엄 제거(-
10%)로 목 표 주 가 를 260,000원으로 하향. 그러나, RA 설립에서의 동사 지분 
참여 및 기말 배당(’26년 DPS 7,000원 추정)에 따른 상 대 적 배당 수익 매력
도 부각 등은 주가 모 멘 텀 에 있어 기대 가능한 요소.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 10월 8 일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 8

기아 (000270) [한화리서치]

8 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 9 월 30 일) 
투자등급 매수 중립 매도 합계

### Page 1

현대차 (005380)

EARNINGS PREVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.10.16  
4분기 반등 기대

3Q26 Preview 
현대차는 3Q26E 매출액 42.4조원(-9.4% YoY, -13.9% QoQ), 영업이익 2.5조원(-
2.8% YoY, -13.5% QoQ, OPM 5.8%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회할 전망이다. 각 부문
별 영업이익은 자동차 1.7조원(-2.0% YoY, -14.9% QoQ, OPM 4.8%), 금융 5,501억
원(-4.5% YoY, -27.5% QoQ, OPM 6.9%)으로 예상한다. 
생산 정상화 및 신차 확대를 통한 수익성 개선 기대 
현대차는 글로벌 도매 판매 91.8만대(-11.5% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 90.9
만대(-9.6%)를 기록했다. 7~8월에 발생한 파업 영향으로 약 5.5만대 규모의 생산 차
질이 발생했다. 파업 영향으로 HEV 판매 증가율이 1%에 그치면서, HEV 판매 확대에 
따른 믹스 개선 효과도 시장 기대에 미치지 못했다.  
4분기부터는 3분기 파업 영향으로 지연된 생산량 회복 및 신차 확대가 본격화되면서, 
전분기 대비 매출 및 수익성 개선이 기대된다. 동사는 보급형 전기차 아이오닉3의 튀
르키예 생산을 9월부터 시작했으며, 이를 통해 유럽 시장 판매량은 점차 회복될 전망
이다. 또한, 1) HMGMA를 통한 HEV 미국 현지 생산 확대, 2) 제네시스 HEV 모델 순
차적 출시를 통해 내년부터 북미를 중심으로 믹스 개선이 본격화될 전망이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 520,000원으로 하향 
2027년 영업이익은 11.2조원(+10.2%)를 기록할 것으로 예상된다. 원화 약세 지속을 
반영해 이익 추정치를 하향하며, 이에 따라 목표주가를 하향한다. 다만, 현재 주가는 
12M Fwd P/E 기준 6.1배로 Legacy OEM 평균(7.7배)보다 낮은 수준이다. 1) RMAC 
성과 구체화, 2) RA(로봇 생산법인) 착공 등 Physical AI 사업이 구체화될 경우 밸류에
이션 할증이 가능할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
아직 수요의 끝은 보이지 않는다 
AI 투자에 대한 우려의 목소리도 있지만 정작 부품을 공급하고 투자하는 쪽의 목소
리는 시간이 갈수록 더 커지고 있다. 공급 부족의 시점 매 분기 뒤로 밀려나고 있기 
때문이다. 28년까지 공급부족이라고 했던 메모리업체들은 이구동성으로 30년까지 공
급부족이 지속된다고 언급하고 있다. 이러한 변화는 실적에 고스란히 반영되고 있
다. 26년 3분기 메모리 영업이익만 108.5조원으로 추정한다. 4 분기에는 140조원에 
이를 것으로 전망한다. 
HBM4와 QLC는 차별화 포인트 
삼성전자는 HBM에서 입지가 좋았던 적이 없었다. 점유율, 수익성, 가동률, 제품 성
능, 고객 등 모든 항목에서 경쟁사 대비 열위에 있었다. HBM4는 모든 항목에서 입
지를 개선시킬 변수가 될 것으로 예상한다. 26년 3분기 물량은 경쟁사 수준일 것으
로 보이고 연간 물량도 유사한 수준일 전망이다. 27년에는 가격 인상과 가동률 상승
으로 수익성도 크게 개선될 것으로 기대한다. QLC는 TLC와 함께 AI 모델 진화의 
수혜를 받을 제품으로 빠르게 부상하고 있다. 메타의 Muse 영향이다. 접속자당 가
상 메모리를 NAND에 100GB를 할당하고 있기 때문이다.  
부족한 주주환원으로 투자 우선 순위에서 밀릴 듯 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 이는 여러 변수에도 메모리 성장은 
변화가 없을 것으로 전망하고, 메모리 수요는 장기적 성장 국면에 진입했고, HBM 
시장 내 입지가 강화되고 있고, 현재 주가는 실적 대비 지나치게 저평가된 수준으로 
판단하기 때문이다. 목표주가는 350,000원으로 하향한다. 현재 수익성과 시장 상황
을 고려하면 적정 주가는 460,000원으로 판단하지만 주주환원 정책이 시장 기대치
에 미치지 못하고, 경쟁사와 비교해도 선호도가 낮아질 것을 고려하면 주가 상승은 
제한적일 것으로 판단하기 때문이다.

### Page 2

삼성전자 (005930)

2 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 3분기 매출액은 195.3조원 
삼성전자의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 13.9% 증가한 195.3조원이다. VD/
가전을 제외한 주요 사업부 매출액은 2분기 대비 증가했다. DS, MX 모두 물량보다는 
가격 효과가 더 크게 작용한 것으로 분석된다. 사업부별로는  
1) DS 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 15.4% 증가한 147.1조원이다. 메모리
는 26년 2분기 대비 16.4% 증가했다. 26년 3분기 DRAM B/G(Bit Growth)
는 +2.0%, ASP는 +20.0%이다. NAND B/G는 +5.0%, ASP는 +18.0%이다. 
HBM 물량 증가는 HBM4가 주도했고, 그 이하 제품 비중은 하락한 것으로 
추정한다.  
2) 디스플레이 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 7.1% 증가한 8조원이다. 소형
은 해외 고객 및 국내 고객의 신제품 출시 영향으로 ASP 상승, 물량 증가 
효과가 동반되었고, 대형은 게이밍 모니터 비중을 확대했다. 
3) MX/네트워크 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 13.4% 증가한 37.7조원이
다. MX는 15.5% 증가했다. 물량은 소폭 감소했고, ASP 상승 영향을 받았다. 
네트워크 사업부 매출액은 2분기 대비 60% 감소했다.  
4) VD/가전 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 4.1% 감소한 13.9조원이다. VD 
매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 6.5% 감소했다. 가전은 26년 2분기 대비 1.3% 
감소했다.

김운호  6915-5656

IBKS RESEARCH │3 
26년 3분기 영업이익은 107.4조원 
삼성전자의 26년 3분기 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 20% 증가한 107.4조원이다. DS,  
디스플레이와 MX는 2분기 대비 증가했고, VD/가전은 2분기 대비 감소했다. 사업부별
로는 
1) DS 사업부는 26년 2분기 대비 19.4% 증가한 106.5조원이다. 2분기 대비 영
업이익률은 소폭 상승했다. 메모리 영업이익은 2분기 대비 19.0% 증가했고, 
비메모리는 영업적자를 유지했다.  
2) 디스플레이 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 54.5% 증가한 1.1조원이다. 
중소형 신제품 효과로 수익성이 개선된 영향이다. 대형도 믹스 개선으로 수
익성이 개선되었다. 
3) MX/네트워크 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 적자폭이 감소했다. 물량
은 줄었지만 High End 비중 상승으로 ASP는 개선되었고, 원가 비중이 낮아
진 영향이다. 
4) VD/가전 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 적자 규모가 커졌다.

### Page 6

삼성전자 (005930)

6 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 4분기 매출액은 226.5조원으로 예상 
삼성전자의 26년 4분기 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 16.0% 증가한 226.5조원으로 예상
한다. DS가 증가하고 나머지는 부진할 것으로 예상한다.  
DS는 메모리가 성장을 주도할 것으로 기대한다. 가격과 물량 모두 3분기 대비 증가할 
것으로 전망한다. Context Server에 대한 수요가 증가하고, Personal Agent의 가상메
모리 설정으로 인해 NAND 수요가 증가하면서 QLC에 대한 수요 증가 속도가 빨라질 
전망이다. 이로 인해 NAND Bit Growth는 경쟁사와 차별화된 수준일 것으로 예상한
다.  
HBM 물량도 꾸준히 증가할 것으로 기대한다. 3 분기에도 HBM4를 중심으로 물량이 
크게 증가했다. HBM4는 SK하이닉스와 비슷한 물량일 것으로 예상한다. 아직 전체 
물량은 SK하이닉스가 더 많은 상황이고, HBM 내 HBM4 비중은 업계 최고 수준인 것
으로 판단한다. 26년까지는 HBM이 수익성에는 마이너스 요인이지만, 27년에는 
DRAM 수준의 영업이익이 가능할 것으로 기대한다.  
MX는 비수기 저가 라인 물량 조정으로 전체 물량은 감소하지만, 제품믹스는 개선되
어 판가는 3분기 대비 상승할 것으로 예상한다.

26년 4분기 영업이익은 138.2조원으로 예상 
삼성전자의 26년 4분기 영업이익은 26년 3분기 대비 28.7% 증가한 138.2조원으로 예
상한다. DS 사업부를 제외하고 모두 3분기 대비 부진할 것으로 예상한다.  
DS 사업부 영업이익은 3분기 대비 30% 증가한 138.5조원으로 전망한다. DRAM, 
NAND 가격이 10% 초중반 수준으로 상승하고, 물량은 10% 중반 이상 증가하면서 3
분기 대비 영업이익률 개선을 기대하기 때문이다. HBM 물량 증가가 이익 개선에 미
치는 영향은 제한적일 것으로 예상한다.  
디스플레이, MX는 물량 부진으로 3분기 대비 영업이익은 부진할 것으로 전망한다.

DS가 성장 주도 
DS만 보자

### Page 7

김운호  6915-5656

IBKS RESEARCH │7 
Check Point 
1. 모자란 듯한 주주환원 
26년 3분기 실적 발표에서 밝힌 주주환원 정책은 지난 8월의 내용과 다르지 않다. 3
분기 배당 30조원(주당 4,601원)을 확정한 정도이다. 잔여 배당 재원이 55~75조원으
로 구체화했고, 현금 배당과 자사주 매입을 병행하겠다고 밝혔다.  
자사주 매입 시 우선주 매입도 고려 대상이라고 언급한 것은 고무적이지만 27년부터 
새롭게 시작되는 주주환원 정책을 결정하기 위한 이사회 개최는 27년 1월에 열릴 것
으로 예고하였기에 연내 추가적인 주주환원 발표는 없을 것으로 판단한다.  
2. HBM4로 HBM 시장에서 입지 강화 
HBM4를 통해서 자신감을 회복하고 있는 것으로 판단한다. 양산 시점은 경쟁사 대비 
빨랐고, 3분기 물량은 경쟁사와 비슷한 수준인 것으로 추정한다. HBM3e가 부진했기 
때문에 전체 생산량에서 HBM에서 HBM4 비중은 가장 높은 수준일 것으로 추정한다.  
26년 전체 물량도 경쟁사와 비슷한 수준이 될 것으로 전망한다. 삼성전자는 HBM 시
장 점유율을 DRAM 수준으로 맞추겠다는 목표를 3분기에 달성한 것으로 밝혔다.  
물량이 증가하면서 가동률도 빠르게 개선될 것으로 기대한다. 아직은 DRAM과 영업
이익률에서 큰 차이가 있지만 27년에는 인상된 가격, 높아진 수율, 가동률 개선으로 
DRAM과 영업이익률 차이를 좁힐 수 있을 것으로 기대한다.  
3. QLC NAND 시장 확대 
AI 컴퓨팅 확산은 NAND에게도 성장 기회이다. AI 모델들의 Context Window가 길어
져 KV Cache 메모리에 대한 수요가 커지면서 High Endurance TLC 수요가 급증하고 
있다. DRAM을 대체할 수준의 NAND가 필요하게 되었기 때문이다.  
이 뿐만 아니라 고용량 NAND에 대한 수요도 Personal Agent의 출현으로 급속히 증
가할 것으로 기대한다. 여기에는 QLC NAND가 주로 사용된다. 메타의 Muse는 접속
자 1인에 대해서 DRAM 8GB, NAND 100GB의 가상 메모리를 설정하고 운영한다.  
Agent AI의 확산은 스토리지에 대한 수요도 급격히 확대할 것으로 기대하고, 삼성전
자에게도 기회가 될 것으로 기대한다.  
26년 4분기 NAND Bit Growth는 삼성전자가 가장 높을 것으로 예상한다.

삼성전자 (005930)

8 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 350,000원으로 하향 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 이는 
1) 여러 변수에도 메모리 성장은 변화가 없을 것으로 전망하고, 
2) 메모리 수요는 장기적 성장 국면에 진입했고, 
3) HBM 시장 내 입지가 강화되고 있고, 
4) 현재 주가는 실적 대비 지나치게 저평가된 수준으로 판단하기 때문이다. 
목표주가는 350,000원으로 하향한다. 현재 수익성과 시장 상황을 고려하면 적정 주가
는 460,000원으로 판단하지만 주주환원 정책이 시장 기대치에 미치지 못하고, 경쟁사
와 비교해도 선호도가 낮아질 것을 고려하면 주가 상승은 제한적일 것으로 판단하기 
때문이다.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
We reaffirm our expectation  that the RBA Monetary Policy
Board will again increase the cash rate at the November
meeting. Today’s inflation data and Senate testimony are
the final pieces of information before the RBA Monetary
Policy Board (MPB) meets next week. Recent data and

communication have consistently pointed to the staff
recommending an increase in the cash rate. We expect a
majority of the external members to agree, though some
members might dissent.
Inflation is above target, and above what the RBA’s August
forecasts implied. Some upside risks identified in August have
partially materialised, particularly energy prices. The RBA is
especially worried that above-target inflation will become
embedded in people’s expectations and pricing decisions. It

will therefore deem that further policy tightening is needed.
The RBA has made it clear that it is down-weighting the recent
easing in the labour market, even though this was more than it
forecast. In today’s opening statement  to the Senate hearing,
the Governor said, “Despite the unemployment rate recently
rising a little more than we had expected, the labour market
seems a little tighter than we had previously judged. The
evidence for this is elevated underlying inflationary pressures.

That is, when we exclude some of the large volatile price
movements such as fuel we see inflationary pressure in areas
that reflect domestic demand for goods and services.” In the
Senate, the Governor also said that they were not worried
about the current pace of increase in unemployment.
Of course, this takes no account of the lagged relationship
between the labour market and inflation. The argument is that
if that measure of inflation last quarter was too high, it must

therefore be that the labour market is too tight. Nor does it
allow sufficiently for non-labour cost shocks, which business
surveys indicate are the real issue. But whether this judgement
by the RBA turns out to be right or not will not be revealed
for some time, certainly not soon enough to change its mind
before next week. And while some MPB members might have
a more optimistic view about labour supply trends than the
RBA staff, they are likely to be a minority.

The RBA is also relatively sanguine about the current
downturn in the housing market. The Governor and other
RBA officials are at pains to highlight that they do not target
housing prices; nor should they. The RBA considers that most,
though not all, of the current downswing is an expected effect
of tighter monetary policy.
RBA to hike in November, as inflation remains too high
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The question then turns to the future beyond November. While

we still expect a hawkish tone in the RBA’s communication
until inflation has clearly turned down, further hikes beyond
next week are not currently our base case. Clearly, though, the
risks of another hike – most likely in February – have increased.
Our current forecast for Q4 inflation would seem to give them
cover for such a move. We note, however, that home-building
costs in the CPI have undershot expectations in the past couple
of months. This might imply a different view of the extent of

domestic demand pressures than the RBA currently believes.
However, a lot can happen in three months, as we have just
seen. The conflict in the Middle East  could escalate further,
lifting energy prices beyond our current base case. It could
also go the other way, though, if the US and Iran manage to
strike a post-midterm-election deal. And as their counterparts
in New Zealand highlighted today, one risk is that the global
economy weakens suddenly. In the post-meeting statement ,

the RBNZ Monetary Policy Committee said, “the world
economy could tip abruptly into a downturn causing weaker
inflation over the medium term.” This risk was something the
RBNZ MPC thought could crystallise in the next six weeks.
Whether on this timetable or something more drawn out, this
possibility does not appear to be on the RBA’s radar. Nor is it
our base case, though we have previously worried that the AI
financing boom could hit a wall quite suddenly.

“We reaffirm our expectation that the RBA
Monetary Policy Board will again increase the
cash rate at the November meeting"

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: the source and persistence of inflation
will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related
risks through 2027; although, in the absence of another shock,
we expect them to remain on hold.
The Q3 GDP report certainly supported Chair Warsh’s view

on the breadth of growth, the 3.4% annualised gain for GDP
benefitting from a 3.8% annualised increase in household
consumption, 12.7% growth in non-residential investment
as well as a robust increase in government spending.
Notably though, the contribution from business investment
was effectively offset by net exports’ drag given most of
the componentry related to AI infrastructure investment is
imported. This is not to say that US firms won’t benefit from

the investment once complete, but simply that much of the
initial return goes to the foreign businesses who provide the
necessary capital equipment.
On consumption, the monthly PCE data implies downside risks
remain for the consumer, the increase in nominal spending
seen in the month requiring dissaving and, on a real basis,
consumption flat in the month following a 0.5% gain in
August. Cost-of-living pressures and uncertainty over the
outlook is expected to see household consumption growth

average around trend over the coming year, albeit with
considerable volatility quarter to quarter. This should see PCE
inflation slow back towards its 2.0%yr medium-term target as
the FOMC desires, from 3.6%yr and 3.1%yr for the headline
and core measures respectively as of September.
Across the Atlantic, the ECB also left its key policy
rates unchanged in October. Of note from the meeting
communications, energy prices continue to pose upside risks

for inflation, but also downside risks for growth. "While the
full effects of the energy shock are still unfolding, underlying
inflation has remained broadly stable so far and wages do not
show a material response". "Financing conditions have [also]
tightened", "which is likely to weigh on inflation and growth".
In the press conference, President Lagarde noted that,
between the October and December meetings, a significant
amount of new economic information will be released,

allowing forecasts and risks assessments to be tested.
Based on the October meeting communications, we continue
to expect a rate hike at the December meeting but believe this
will be prove to be the last hike of the cycle. Note though, the
spectrum of risks Europe faces is broad and skewed towards
inflation in the near term, hence the Council’s guidance will
remain hawkish. President Lagarde also announced today that
she will step down as ECB President in February ahead of the

scheduled conclusion of her term next October.
In Australia this week, all eyes were on the September CPI .
Headline inflation rose 0.8% (4.3%yr) in the month, largely
owing to a 9.4% jump in fuel prices and a seasonal surge in
domestic and international holiday travel & accommodation.
The underlying inflation pulse also proved hotter than
anticipated for Q3 overall, up 1.0% (3.7%yr).
While some parts of the basket are seeing disinflation –
housing in particular looks to be cooling faster than expected

as builders lure sales with discounts – other components are
still showing persistence. Highlighting this is market services
ex volatiles, which the RBA watches closely as an indicator
of price pressures related to domestic capacity. In Q3, this
metric rose 1.2% (3.2%yr), and the monthly detail points
to momentum holding up – in line with our expectations for
another chunky read in Q4.
With inflation running well above the RBA’s August forecasts,

and some of the energy risks flagged then materialising, we
continue to expect the Monetary Policy Board will deliver a 25bp
rate hike to 4.85% in November. Beyond November, another rate
hike in February is a risk, but is not currently part of our base case.
Across the Tasman, the RBNZ this week surprised market
expectations for a 25bp rate hike, deciding instead to wait for
further information before taking the next step with policy. The
decision was made 4:3, with the Governor in the ‘on hold’ camp. A

25bp hike in December to 3.00% and further tightening in 2027
(Westpac NZ forecasts a peak of 4.00% in September) seems
probable; however, each decision will depend on fuel prices,
inflation expectations and evidence of second-round pricing
pressures, along with the evolution of global economic risks.
In the US, the FOMC also held rates steady  at its October
meeting. Based on prior comments by key members, the
decision was not a surprise, and neither were Governor Cook

and Cleveland Fed President Hammack’s dissents in favour
of a 25bp hike. The Committee remains positive on economic
activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding at
a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in September). Chair
Warsh also subsequently emphasised the breadth of economic
growth outside of tech-related investment (evinced by Q3
GDP the day after; see below).
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in
the statement. However, inflation “remains elevated” and the

FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable
return of annual inflation to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target.
This guidance and the “accumulation of more information will
help the Committee determine the policy stance” suggests
another 25bp hike is likely in December. Thereafter, the FOMC
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
A lesson to be learned
The RBNZ delivered the biggest shock to markets outside of
a crisis period in over 20 years this week by leaving the OCR
unchanged. Markets had priced around a 98% probability of
a 25bp increase, and all major domestic analysts concurred.

After the CPI data the week earlier, we belatedly shifted our
call to a hike despite plenty of good arguments in favour of
the RBNZ sticking to its plan to pause. With the benefit of
hindsight, too much weight was put on market pricing and
short-term inflation drivers and not enough on the medium
term. That’s an important lesson to be learned.
The highlight of an action-packed week of central banking news
was the unexpected decision of the RBNZ to leave the OCR at

2.75%, despite near unanimous analyst expectations and market
pricing in favour of a 25bp hike. We had shifted sides from a
hold to a hike just a week earlier, in the wake of a CPI outcome
that was weaker than RBNZ expectations on the surface, but
with some evidence of firm core inflation undertones.
While we clearly cannot claim to have gotten this call right,
we always thought that the outcome was not the “done deal”
that markets had priced in. For example, in our preview of the

meeting we noted:
“We still think a more measured tightening approach is
appropriate from here. Raising the OCR at every meeting
until something ‘breaks’ could be a risky strategy if downside
growth concerns crystalise.”
And we saw a vote on the decision as very possible:
“A vote seems very possible as differences in view will likely
remain among the MPC about how much the medium-term
inflation outlook has really shifted in recent weeks.”
The 4:3 vote that was ultimately required to bring the MPC

to a decision is a testament to just how finely balanced the
decision was. We had thought that the hike was perhaps
around a 60-70% probability whereas the Record of Meeting
implies something more like a 50:50 call.
In the final analysis, the RBNZ has merely followed through on
the pause it signalled for this meeting back in the September
Monetary Policy Statement. All MPC members continue to see
a case for a higher OCR in time, but there are different views

around the timing of those increases. Governor Breman noted
that further interest rate increases remain likely in coming
meetings but are conditional on the whether the outlook
evolves in line with expectations. Indeed, the Governor noted
in later comments to press that a December hike was a “high”
probability and that the outlook remains for OCR “hikes” as the
base case. This suggests that the RBNZ’s view on the terminal
OCR hasn’t really shifted down from the 3.25% level presented

back in September. And it may be reasonable to speculate,
subject to data evolving as the RBNZ expects, that a modest
upgrade in those forward OCR forecasts could be in the offing.
So how did we all get this so wrong? This outcome was a real
shock given the historical record shows that over the history of
the OCR the market has without exception correctly predicted
a 25bp hike when the implied probability built into markets
was as high as it was. Clearly some recalibration of the model

is required. We can identify two key areas where lessons
should be learned: too much weight on market pricing; and not
enough on drivers of medium-term inflation.
Market pricing did not adequately reflect the
MPC’s desire to shift strategy .
Firstly, and most importantly, we put too much weight on
market pricing and too little on the RBNZ’s clearly stated
strategy of preferring a pause at the October meeting to
assess the impact of interest rate increases already done. In our

Preview we incorrectly surmised that silence equated to assent
and that market pricing of a hike would make an OCR hike the
less disruptive option – especially given the sense that a hike
to 3% was still likely in December as implied by the RBNZ’s
September forecasts. From a macroeconomic perspective,
raising rates six weeks early is of little consequence.
This logic, as sound as it was, was wrong, as market pricing did
not reflect a large enough change in the medium-term inflation

outlook to justify a change in RBNZ strategy. Markets at least
partially held the view, based on a long history of revealed
RBNZ preference, that the RBNZ keeps moving the OCR in the
same direction until confronted with compelling evidence that
a halt to the cycle is in prospect. That operating approach may
be under review now.
It’s not uncommon for other central banks to take breaks
while amid an interest rate cycle to gather information on
the impact of work already done before moving forward.

This week’s pause by the FOMC in the face of a fierce set of
monthly inflation readings in the last month is an example.
Pausing does not need to imply stopping or reversing. Indeed,
taking a more measured approach could prove to be solid
risk management – as we identified in our MPR preview note.
Risk management concerns probably should have greater
prominence in this cycle given we are seeing a measure of
Kelly Eckhold
Chief Economist NZ

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
supply side cost shocks that might not be persistent. The
Statement of Record contains a wealth of material related to
these sorts of uncertainties and concerns. Markets were not
listening to these concerns, however.
An insufficient focus on drivers of medium-term

inflation
Markets were not listening to the alarm bells because there
was a heavy focus on factors that may be more relevant to the
short-term inflation outlook than medium-term inflation. Top
of mind were the rise in energy prices and fall in the exchange
rate. Both factors look set to significantly boost the short-
term inflation outlook – for example we currently forecast
December quarter annual inflation of 4.3%, with upside risks
from there if fuel prices do not fall over the balance of the

quarter. However, our analysis of the data surprises suggested
no significant shift to the implied RBNZ OCR track one year
ahead. Upward pressure on the future OCR projection from
the lower exchange rate and stronger GDP was balanced
by higher long term interest rates and the lower Q3 CPI
outcome. Higher energy prices were not seen as a key factor
as they don’t impact medium term inflation unless inflation
expectations are dislodged (no evidence was found of that

since September) or if the increase in energy prices is viewed
as persistent (futures markets continue to imply lower forward
energy prices, although MPC hawks clearly see risks of
persistent inflation coming from supply shocks).
The RBNZ’s communications at the MPR and in its aftermath
are directing markets to pay more attention to medium-term
inflation drivers in the future. Hence the references to the
strength of demand and inflation expectations. The risk to

the RBNZ’s inflation forecasts are to the upside, and hence
we might reasonably expect some upgrade of the OCR profile
when we see new forecasts in December. But it’s not a done
deal by any means as data and events between now and then
will dictate what stance the RBNZ takes. This week’s business
and consumer confidence reports do not seem to present many
smoking guns of imminent second-round inflation pressures,
even though business and consumer sentiment seems to be

taking the higher oil price in its stride.
Is core inflation high enough yet to shift the
strategy?
Another interesting point made by the Governor was that the
markets (and analysts) misinterpreted how the September
quarter CPI was read by the RBNZ, which evidently took
greater comfort from the outcome. Governor Breman has noted
she wished the RBNZ has protocols in place to communicate
with markets the RBNZ’s view on that CPI data given that it
seems to have been a factor that figured importantly in at least

some MPC member’s thinking. Headline inflation was lower
than the RBNZ expected and importantly the core inflation
measures, that we had thought might have been of concern to
the MPC, do not yet seem to have been at a level sufficient to
shift the RBNZ from its pause-then-hike strategy. We remain
sceptical that a 3.25% OCR will prove sufficient to bring core
inflation down significantly. But core inflation appears to need
to lift for the MPC to become more agitated. It likely will lift,

but we won’t get more data on that until January 2027.
December hike likely but not a done deal
We expect a further 25bp hike at this year’s final meeting on 9
December and then 25bp increases in the February and March
2027 meetings, followed by a slower pace of increases at the
June and September Monetary Policy Statement meetings.
Our forecast of a peak OCR of 4% from September 2027 has
remained unchanged throughout, as has our assessment of a
3.75% neutral OCR.

That said, in the current uncertain environment, it goes without
saying that the evolution of monetary policy will depend on
how both global events and key data evolve. Therefore, as the
RBNZ noted itself, the exact timing of the tightening profile
is highly uncertain and even the tightening we forecast at the
December meeting should not be regarded as a done deal.
Looking ahead, the ‘hardest’ data to come will be the Q3
labour market surveys where we anticipate a small fall in

the unemployment rate to 5.5% but still moderate wage
pressures. The December quarter RBNZ inflation expectations
surveys will be of significant interest (17, 20 and 24
November) for MPC members looking for persistent inflation
impacts. The usual run of monthly activity and selected prices
indicators will be useful in narrowing down Q4 growth and
inflation momentum. And finally, the MPC’s focus on external
risks will imply keeping an eye on global fixed income, equity

and credit markets for signs of imminent calamity.
Finally, November 7 is the general election. A close race is
envisaged that could impact on the RBNZ’s mandate. We
will be offering client briefings on Monday 9 November and
Tuesday 10 November to outline the immediate implications
of the election outcome for markets and the economy. Contact
your relationship manager for details.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: RBA Policy Decision (%)
Nov 3, Last: 4.60, Westpac f/c: 4.85
Market f/c: 4.85, Range: 4.60 to 4.85
We reaffirm our expectation that the RBA Monetary Policy
Board (MPB) will decide to raise the cash rate by 25bps to
4.85% at the November meeting.

Inflation is above target, and above what the RBA’s August
forecasts implied. Some upside risks identified in August have
partially materialised, particularly energy prices. The RBA is
especially worried that above-target inflation will become
embedded in people’s expectations and pricing decisions.
Recent data and communication have consistently pointed to
the RBA staff recommending an increase in the cash rate. We
expect a majority of the external board members to agree,

though some members might dissent.
AUS: Oct Cotality Home Value Index (%mth)
Nov 2, Last: –1.1, Westpac f/c: –1.3
The Cotality home value index – covering all of Australia – fell
1.1% in Sep with downward revisions shaving a further 0.4%
off prices. The correction has now seen prices decline 5.2%
from their March peak, annual growth stalling flat. Cotality
reported 97% of capital city suburbs experienced falls over the
three months to September.
The October update is set to be another weak one with an

added hit coming from the RBA’s interest rate rise in late
September. The daily measure (covering the five major capital
cities rather than all of Australia) is tracking a 1.5% decline vs
1.3% in September. The detail points to slightly bigger falls
in Sydney, Brisbane and Perth with Melbourne continuing
to record more moderate declines. Note that seasonality is
usually a slight positive in the October month.
AUS: Sep Dwelling Approvals (%mth)
Nov 2, Last: –6.1, Westpac f/c: –7.0

Market f/c: 2.0, Range: –7.0 to 5.0
Dwelling approvals fell 6.1%mth in August, with the annual
pace easing slightly to 10.3%yr. Weakness centred on unit
approvals with private detached house approvals actually
posting a solid rebound from a July month fall, touching a new
cycle high (up 18.4%yr).
We expect the September update to show a much clearer
weakening. New construction is coming under pressure
from multiple fronts: interest rate rises; a sharp downturn in

established markets; and rising costs – with a great deal of
uncertainty around each of these. New home sales have fell
sharply through the September quarter and we expect to
see detached house approvals show something similar. Unit
approvals are coming off a weak read and should be more
stable. Total approvals are expected to print 7% lower.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Sep Goods Trade Balance ($bn)
Nov 5, Last: 0.5, Westpac f/c: 2.0
Market f/c: 1.0, Range: –1.0 to 2.5
Australia’s goods trade surplus narrowed to $0.5bn in August.
Recently, the trade balance has been oscillating between
small deficits and surpluses with significant volatility in the

underlying trade mix.
Swings in non-monetary gold, fuel and cars have all played
a large role, but the most eye-catching result from Aug was
the 79% surge in automatic data processing equipment (ADP)
imports, which captures the investment upswing related to
data centres.
For September, we expect an increase in the surplus as the
ADP import surge unwinds, though we are likely in store for
ongoing monthly volatility in trade for some time.
NZ: Sep Building Consents (%mth)

Nov 3, Last: +5.6%, Westpac f/c: Flat
Annual residential building consent numbers are currently at
a three year high, and we expect that they will remain firm in
the September update. However, monthly consent numbers
are showing signs of peaking, with falls in three of the last four
months. We’re watching to see if that trend has continued,
particularly given the ongoing softness in the housing
market.  In the non-residential space, we’ve seen softness

in the number of retail and office developments, but greater
resilience in the industrial and storage segment.
AUS: Sep Household Spending Indicator (%mth)
Nov 4, Last: flat, Westpac f/c: –0.5
Market f/c: 0.2, Range: –0.5 to 0.8
The household spending indicator was unchanged in August,
annual growth easing to 6.8%yr. The detail showed a modest
rotation away from discretionary categories towards non-
discretionary.
Our Westpac-DataX Card Tracker  showed a decent rise in

the September month although new vehicle sales (which are
not covered by the tracker but are in scope for the spending
indicator) look to have posted a decline. Overall we expect
the indicator to show a 0.5% decline in the September month.
Note that the update with also include Q3 estimates of real
spending – we expect this to show a 0.6%qtr gain with prices
up 1.2%qtr and nominal spend up 1.8%qtr.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Q3 Labour Market Surveys (%)
Nov 4, Unemployment Rate, Last: 5.6, Westpac f/c: 5.5
LCI Private Sector, Last: 0.7, Westpac f/c: 0.5
We expect the unemployment rate to edge down to 5.5%
for the September quarter. Job numbers have been gaining
momentum in recent months, and now look to be enough to

outstrip population growth. However, the existing slack in the
labour market is likely to be keeping wage growth subdued,
despite cost-of-living pressures on workers. Our forecasts are
a little more positive overall than what the RBNZ assumed in
its September MPS forecasts.
US: Oct Employment Report
Nov 6, Payrolls, Last: 29k, Market f/c: 55k, WBC f/c: 75k
Nov 6, U/E rate, Last: 4.2%, Market f/c: 4.2%, WBC f/c: 4.3%
Nonfarm payrolls have shown considerable volatility of late,

July’s -10k followed by a 133k gain in August then a 29k
increase in September. However, the 3-month average of 51k
is likely a good guide on underlying momentum. In October, we
look for a 75k gain, although we expect some of that increase
to be offset by a modest reduction to the prior two months,
keeping the 3-month average around 50k.
While the participation rate has lifted in recent months, it
remains well below the average of 2025. Month-to-month, a

change either way is not guaranteed, but it seems likely that
the participation rate will trend higher in coming months. We
therefore expect the unemployment rate to edge up.

MACRO STRATEGY
The Five Shocks of 2026—And the Extraordinary Resilience of the U.S. Economy
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
This is the year the consensus got mugged.
Rewind to the start of the year. The market consensus was that the U.S. economy was
set to slow. The Fed was set to cut interest rates. Corporate earnings were set to grow—

but not explode. Energy prices would remain tame. And the odds looked good that the
Republicans would control the House, the Senate, or both, following the midterm election.
Nine months on, the landscape looks starkly different. Defined by five shocks—energy,
interest rates, earnings, growth, and politics—2026 looks nothing like the year investors
thought they were buying on January 1.
Shock 1: Diesel Matters. While global oil prices have moved higher since the start of

the U.S./Iran war on February 28, oil prices have yet to surpass their previous record high
because the world has released some 400 million barrels from strategic reserves this year,
China has curbed its demand for oil imports, and the U.S. has goosed its energy exports to
record highs.
But here is the problem: While gasoline powers the American consumer, diesel powers the
American economy—and diesel prices are now sitting at record highs. Diesel supplies have

been hammered by wars in the Middle East and Ukraine that have sharply reduced refining
capacity. According to the International Energy Agency, by August, exports of diesel from
refiners in the Middle East had fallen by a quarter from prewar levels. Meanwhile, Ukraine
has hit 24 of Russia’s 34 largest refineries, reducing supplies from one of the world’s
largest diesel exporters. The upshot: U.S. diesel prices have soared from $3.55 a gallon
at the start of the year to roughly $6.52 a gallon. While crude oil trades for around $100

a barrel, the retail equivalent of a barrel of diesel now costs more than $260. The diesel
shock of 2026 was the last thing investors saw coming—and remains a key impediment to
structurally lower inflation.
Shock 2: From Interest Rate Cuts to Rate Hikes. Perhaps no other consensus view
entering 2026 has been turned more dramatically on its head than expectations for the
Fed. Heading into 2026, markets were pricing in rate cuts on expectations of a cooling

labor market, moderating inflation, and slower economic growth. The Fed easing cycle was
expected to continue in 2026. In January 2026, futures showed a roughly 50% chance of a
cut by March.
Expectations, however, flipped in late February with the start of the U.S./Iran war. The
Middle East energy shock reignited inflationary pressures, which, combined with stronger-
than-expected U.S. economic growth and a tightening labor market, have demolished the

odds of easier money in 2026. Short- and long-term Treasury yields rose sharply last week
in the wake of the S&P Global Flash U.S. Composite Purchasing Managers’ Index (PMI)
which pointed to the strongest business expansion in more than five years and the fastest
employment growth in over four years, while also reinforcing expectations that the Fed
will maintain a restrictive policy stance for longer. Markets were expecting two to three
quarter-point rate cuts in 2026; instead, the Fed has already raised rates once this year and

is expected to keep raising rates over the near term.
Shock 3: The Earnings Boom No One Saw Coming. Not all the shocks of 2026 are
negative. Indeed, stronger-than-expected corporate earnings have been among the most
positive factors of this year. Corporate America entered the year facing questions about
tariffs, AI-related capital expenditure spending, the health of the U.S. consumer, operating
leverage and the U.S. dollar outlook. At the start of the year, Wall Street was expecting

roughly $290 of S&P 500 earnings per share for 2026, per Bloomberg estimates. But by
September 21, the estimate had risen by $50, to $340 per share.
Investment Implications
While some of these shocks may
contribute to periods of market
volatility, the combination of strong
earnings growth, robust domestic
demand, and ongoing capital
investment suggests that the
fundamental backdrop for Equities
remains constructive.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Earnings have been upgraded and underpinned by several factors, including robust final 
domestic demand in the U.S., a pickup in global growth, AI-related capital spending, 
productivity gains, and enhanced operating leverage. All these variables have helped deliver 
an earnings “shock” to Wall Street. And as an aside, yes, headline profit growth has been 
inflated by strong technology earnings and one-off investment gains from Amazon and 
Alphabet. That said, the S&P 500’s earnings story remains extraordinary. According to 
FactSet, the 493 companies excluding the Magnificent 7 1 
posted 31.8% earnings growth in 
Q2, the strongest level since late 2021.

Shock 4: An Economy with more Horsepower than the Headlines Suggest. That 
corporate profits have exceeded expectations this ye
ar is due in no small part to an economy 
that continues to surprise to the upside. The Fed was supposed to cut rates this year to 
keep the economy humming and prevent it from losing steam. But rather than limp through 
2026, the economy has leaped ahead. Yes, real gross domestic product (GDP) growth of 
2.1% in Q1 and 1.5% in Q2 are hardly blockbuster annualized rates of growth. However, the 
figures belie the underlying strength of the U.S. economy, with real final domestic demand

(which strips out trade) running at a 4.2% annual rate in Q2. According to the latest reading 
of the Atlanta Fed GDP tracker, Q3 real GDP grow
th is running at a red-hot rate of 5.1%. 
Slowdown? What slowdown?
Resilient consumer spending, robust capital investment, record leve
ls of new business 
formation, and strong foreign direct investment inflows have combined to stoke growth 
in the $32 trillion behemoth called the U.S. economy—the most resilient and dynamic 
economy in the world.
Shock 5: The Unexpected Washington Reset. The last shock is still in the making, but 
given current trends, the odds are rising that the Democrats could control both the House 
and Senate follow
ing the midterm elections. To this point, in January, and per Kalshi, the 
Democrats had a 71% shot at the House and an 18% chance of taking the Senate. Fast-
forward to today, and the odds are a 90% chance that the Democrats take the House and a 
60% chance that they take the Senate.
How all of this plays out remains to be seen—there are some 36 days before the November 
vote, and anything can happen. The market base case remains that the Democrats take the 
House, and the Senate is a toss-up—or too close to call. But with the Democrats expected to 
control at least one chamber of Congress, inv
estors can expect more volatility and uncertainty 
about negotiations ov
er the budget, debt ceiling, defense spending and AI regulation. Another 
political wildcard to watch: the changing dynamics within the Democratic Party.
The Biggest Shock of All for 2026? Pe
rhaps it’s not $6 diesel, rising interest rates, 
solid growth, booming profits or shifting politics. Rather, it’s the ability of the U.S. 
economy to absorb the unexpected, not buckle under stress, and continue generating  wealth 
for investors. To wit, the S&P 500 is on pace for its fourth
 consecutive year of double-digit 
returns.

MARKET VIEW
2026 Has, So Far, Defied the Traditional Midterm Year Playbook
Jordy Fuentes, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Current Landscape. To no surprise, market volatility persisted in 2026. Performance initially
tracked historical midterm election patterns before diverging meaningfully as the year
progressed, though there are still three months to go. Several items remain at hand as U.S.

voters head to the polls in November, including geopolitical and foreign policy uncertainty,
energy driven inflation pressures, affordability concerns, U.S. fiscal trajectory, and rapid
advancements in AI. Regardless of the eventual political outcome, history suggests that
markets and the economy can perform well across different configurations of government.
In our view, markets continue to be anchored by strong fundamentals and encouraging
seasonal trends.

To Be Addressed. U.S. domestic and foreign policy remain in focus as the global reorder,
tit-for-tat tariffs, and geopolitical conflicts spanning the Middle East to Eastern Europe have
added layers of uncertainty to the U.S. economic outlook.
At the same time, persistent geopolitical flare-ups have pushed oil prices materially
higher, adding to inflation pressures and keeping them above the Fed’s 2% target.
Headline Consumer Price Index (CPI) held above 3% year-over-year (YoY) in August, while

its core measure, which excludes food and energy, held at 2.4% YoY. The latest Personal
Consumption Expenditure (PCE), the Fed’s preferred measure of inflation, also remained
above 3% YoY for both its headline and core figures. 2
The U.S. fiscal trajectory is another area of focus. The U.S. budget deficit stands at 5.4% of
GDP , just above this century’s average of 4.6%. Meanwhile, U.S. public sector debt surpassed
the $40 trillion mark for the first time in August, oscillating around 100% of GDP .3 While these

trends warrant close monitoring, we view them as a slow-moving structural headwind rather
than an immediate concern, particularly within a highly innovative and diverse U.S. economy.
AI has emerged as a policy consideration, with debates increasingly centered on both
regulatory safeguards and the economic implications of the expanding infrastructure buildout.
For voters, those concerns extend to affordability, as the significant energy requirements of

new data centers raise questions about potential pressure on electricity costs.
Strap In for The Ride. Leading up to the midterm election, markets tend to experience
greater swings as investors navigate uncertainty surrounding the policy path for the
remainder of the presidential cycle (Exhibit 2A). Since 1930, the S&P 500 has averaged an
intrayear drawdown of 20% in midterm years, with the latest election in 2022 experiencing
a 25% intrayear pullback. This year, however, the S&P 500 largest peak-to-trough drawdown

has been 9%, considerably shallower than its long-term average (Exhibit 2B). Yet while
volatility may rise as election day approaches, 2026 has increasingly defied the traditional
midterm year playbook.
Why 2026 is Different. Despite entering a challenging stretch of the presidential cycle,
U.S. Equities have materially outpaced the typical year two pattern (Exhibit 2C). The S&P
500 experienced its best Q2 in a midterm year since 1938, surging 14.9% compared to an

average decline of 1.6%. From a monthly perspective, September stands out as the weakest
month, though month-to-date performance points to an unusual 1.0% rise. That resilience
follows a 2.6% gain in August, another month in which performance meaningfully exceeded
the historical decline of 0.5% (Exhibit 2D).
Through September, U.S. Equity resilience has also extended beyond headline returns with
both the frequency and magnitude of market pullbacks remaining relatively subdued. Market

corrections of 10% or more have occurred in just 29% of midterm election years since 1930.4
Against this backdrop, the market’s last correction came in spring 2025, while this year has
2 Bloomberg, Bureau of Labor Statistics. Data through August 31, 2026.
3 U.S. Treasury, Bureau of Economic Analysis. Data as of September 21, 2026.
4 Bloomberg, data through December 31, 2022.
Investment Implications
In our view, any related market
weakness could represent a

potential buying opportunity for
long-term investors.

### Page 5

5 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
seen only one 5%+ pullback compared to the average three per year, underscoring relatively
resilient market conditions.5 Year-to-date, the S&P 500 has increased 13%, well above the
average annual return of 3% according to Bloomberg through September 25, and on pace
to mark the strongest midterm election year since 2006. Taken together, 2026 has thus far
defied the traditionally weak midterm year seasonal pattern, reinforcing that every election

cycle is unique and historical trends may be a useful guide to navigate market volatility.
What’s Ahead? Forward performance is increasingly constructive for Equities. In the
post-war period, the S&P 500 rose 100% of the time in the six and 12 months following
elections, averaging 13% and 14%, respectively. 6 Even narrowing the time frame to three
months, markets ended higher 90% of the time. As investors push through the finish line,
the last three months of the year tend to be the strongest with October standing out,

ending higher 67% of the time. On average, Q4 has gained 5.6% since 1930, far surpassing
the preceding three quarters of election years. 7 Greater clarity about the U.S. fiscal policy
path serves as a potential tailwind for investors following the election.
To Take Home. Volatility tends to rise during midterm years, but history suggests that
reacting to short-term political uncertainty has rarely rewarded long-term investors. Over
longer horizons, the data suggests Equity returns and economic strength are shaped less

by election outcomes than by corporate profits, valuations, labor market conditions and
capital investments.

THE TICKER TAPE
What is the bond market telling us?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
We believe the bond market is telling us that the Fed is on track to meeting their dual
mandate of price stability and full employment. More importantly, when examining the
full yield curve the 2-year Treasury yield is currently around 4.88%, which typically is
the most sensitive to the fed funds rate and tends to price in future potential policy

adjustments. At the same time, the 10-year Treasury yield is currently about 5.11%. This
leads us to believe that the market expects the Fed to engineer a couple of more hikes
while the back end of the curve is indicating that economic growth is still healthy.
Taken together, it appears the full curve, which has clearly flattened, believes the Fed is
doing its job, while supply shocks, for obvious reasons, are not leading to higher long-run
inflation. The backup in long-dated yields is growth, not necessarily inflation as indicated

by rising “real” yields. Therefore, we view the drift higher in yields as a more normal sign
than what many have originally suggested. We continue to emphasize higher economic
growth for longer, rising productivity in the medium term, supply shortages to remain in
key areas within the AI space, and shocks in the energy equation. We expect the energy
shocks to ultimately fade away over time, but we do not believe that growth fades
anytime soon. In our view, a more normal bond market pricing in Fed moves at the short

end, and growth at the long end helps financial conditions and economic growth operate
in a more durable manner.
In summary, the front-end of the curve expects 3 to 4 more hikes in the next 18 months
or so. This can be debated obviously based on future data especially short-term energy
supply shocks. Long-end yields are drifting higher based on five main factors below, in
our view.
1. Strong economic growth (both
services and manufacturing)
2. Hawkish Fed

3. Debt and deficit concerns
4. Lower demand for long duration
sovereign debt in general
5. Energy supply shocks
The largest weight being strong economic activity which kick starts portfolio
repositioning particularly as the Fed hikes rates. The repositioning stops once demand
rises again when yields reach attractive levels for the more patient longer term
investment community. The supply shocks ultimately end and debt and deficit worries will
likely remain, but they still take a backseat for now. Longer duration government debt is

becoming more attractive each week even in the face of the concerns above. Be patient

Investment Implications
We continue to expect resilient
economic growth, supported by
productivity gains, AI-related
investment, and a more balanced bond
market that may help sustain favorable
financial conditions.
